Rule where it lands once `.claude/agents` leaves the vendored\n105\t   set, or whether it leaves too.\n106\t6. **Policy-test re-scope.** The vendored `tests/policy.test.ts` must pass in a target whose\n107\t   instruction trees are absent while still binding in scaffold's own tree. Rule the mechanism —\n108\t   pass-on-absence at the assertions named in the evidence, a scaffold-only registration, or\n109\t   another seam — without weakening what it proves where the trees exist.\n110\t7. **The orphan sweep.** Each target needs a one-time deletion of the superseded copies\n111\t   (`PROPOSAL.md:96-100`). Rule whether scaffold ships a mechanism (a verb, a repair extension) or\n112\t   the visit documents a manual step; mechanism-not-product-policy binds.\n113\t8. **Membership edges.** `scripts/*.sh` (bench hooks), `.agents/templates`, `.agents/transports`,\n114\t   `LICENSE`, `guides/guide.md`, `guides/scaffold.md`: instruction surface or tool surface, per\n115\t   `PROPOSAL.md:80-89` and what each governs.\n116\t9. **Documentation parity.** Which guide sections, README passages, and ROADMAP rows the change\n117\t   owns, and what `PROPOSAL.md` itself becomes at acceptance.\n118\t10. **Types-first order and units.** The bounded implementation units, their dependency order, one\n119\t    writer at a time, each with independently checkable acceptance criteria.\n120\t\n121\t## Unknowns\n122\t\n123\t- Whether the cloud harness honors `claudeMdExcludes` and per-root rule loading\n124\t  (`PROPOSAL.md:125-129`): unmeasurable from this session; treat as out of the code change's scope\n125\t  and report it as a standing unknown if your design depends on it.\n126\t- Whether any target consumes `dist/host` paths beyond `audit`/`repair`/`catalog`: report as\n127\t  unknown if load-bearing for your design.\n128\t\n129\t## Scope\n130\t\n131\t**Owned.** None — this is a read-only design lane. Return prose only.\n132\t\n133\t**Shared (report-only).** Every repository file.\n134\t\n135\t**Off-limits.** Editing anything; `.orkestrel/campaign/`; secrets (`.env*`, auth files, tokens).\n136\t\n137\t**Tools and limits.** Read, Grep, Glob only. No file writes, no shell.\n138\t\n139\t## Execution\n140\t\n141\tA native subagent, or a bench engine reading this brief inside its own CLI: perform the assignment\n142\tdirectly and spawn nothing. State which lane you held.\n143\t\n144\t## Output\n145\t\n146\tReturn exactly the `planner` return shape:\n147\t\n148\t- `Design`: the coherent contract — constants, types, helpers, staging, plan, pointer content,\n149\t  test re-scope, documentation — answering every numbered question.\n150\t- `Alternatives`: at most two real alternatives and why the design wins.\n151\t- `Units`: bounded work, each naming role AND engine, ownership, dependencies, and acceptance\n152\t  criteria.\n153\t- `Tensions`: judgment calls named for the other lane to challenge.\n154\t- `Risks`: what could invalidate the design and the evidence that settles each.\n155\t\n156\tDeliver it as your final message. Write no files.\n157\t\n158\t## Deviation contract\n159\t\n160\tA conflict between `PROPOSAL.md` and the law, or evidence that a numbered question rests on a false\n161\tpremise, stops the lane: report expected, found, exact evidence, and one short hypothesis. Where a\n162\tquestion is ancillary — a section's placement, a name between two lawful forms — decide, record,\n163\tand carry on.\n164\t\n165\t## Acceptance criteria\n166\t\n167\t1. The design violates no non-negotiable in `AGENTS.md` and no named rule file.\n170\t4. The exit criterion for the campaign is stated: the enumerated capabilities whose closure ends\n171\t   it.\n172\t\n173\t## Review evidence\n174\t\n175\tThe subject is a design proposal: the proposal is `PROPOSAL.md`, the canon it must satisfy is\n176\t`AGENTS.md` plus the rule files named under Law, and the record of what motivated it is\n177\t`PROPOSAL.md:8-21` with the absorption distillate.\n178\t"}]},"uuid":"55c205b0-d39b-47b6-ba97-e3f4261e5572","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:38:22.263Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5efcb2ea-e95f-48b1-9b8b-7838a083cfce","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}
{"parentUuid":"aef0d79e-0824-44ad-9c44-224b7883c287","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1243f16d-def8-4fe9-8f90-73e8b336c933","agentId":"a889da7e76aca2c24","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LcCULTSYjiQ2KxGsoDUJAC","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# AGENTS.md\n2\t\n3\t> TypeScript · types-first · zero unsolicited dependencies · single-word public APIs.\n4\t> This is the authoritative root for how humans and agents write code in this project.\n5\t\n6\t## Authority and loading\n7\t\n8\t- These instructions apply to every project in this style. Never import assumptions, names, or logic from another repository.\n9\t- The user's current instruction wins. Otherwise this file and its linked rules outrank existing code. Existing code is evidence to verify, not ground truth.\n10\t- `*/types.ts` is authoritative for public APIs. Implementation and tests conform to it. Never undo a user's type edit.\n11\t- `.agents/orchestration.md` governs agent operation only and cannot weaken this coding contract. `CLAUDE.md`, `.codex/config.toml`, and `.cursor/rules/` are harness bridges to it and carry no independent coding policy.\n12\t- `.agents/skills/` contains reusable workflows. An explicitly invoked or dispatch-named skill and its required references are binding process instructions, but cannot weaken this file, the applicable rules, or the governing guide or spec.\n13\t- External delegates, including Cursor and Codex models, have no exemption. Every dispatch restates the non-negotiables, applicable rules, guide or spec, and owned files. Every result receives independent review.\n14\t- Before working, read in order:\n15\t  1. this file;\n16\t  2. every applicable file in `.claude/rules/` from the Rule map section;\n17\t  3. every explicitly invoked or dispatch-named skill and the references it requires;\n18\t  4. `guides/README.md`, the matching guide or spec, and `ROADMAP.md` when present.\n19\t- Rules state **how to write**. Guides and specs state **what to build** and the domain workflow. When they conflict, stop and surface the conflict.\n20\t\n21\t## Project model\n22\t\n23\t```text\n24\tsrc/      published library: core, browser, server, optional styles\n25\tapp/      application: core, browser, server\n26\ttests/    mirrors source; setup*.ts owns shared test infrastructure\n27\tconfigs/  thin target wrappers around root Vite/TypeScript configuration\n28\t```\n29\t\n30\t- `core` is host-independent. Browser and server may import core; core imports neither.\n31\t- `app/core` is host-independent. `app/server` may import app/core plus core and server libraries, never browser code. `app/browser` may import app/core plus core and browser libraries, and reaches server behavior through shared contracts and transports, never through Node or server implementation imports.\n32\t- Enforce environment boundaries with the project toolchain directly: Oxlint restricts declared package, alias, and conventional relative imports; scoped TypeScript projects remove host globals from core and the opposite host; Vite resolves and builds the selected Vue/browser and Node/server graphs; generated-consumer tests exercise those real configurations.\n33\t- Published source never imports private app code. Core stays host-independent. Browser and server stay disjoint.\n34\t- Do not add a second parser or source-language analyzer to duplicate TypeScript, Oxlint, Vue, HTML, CSS, or Vite.\n35\t- `tsconfig.json`, `vite.config.ts`, and each `*/types.ts` are their respective sources of truth.\n36\t- Use only the environments the project needs. Do not delete structural files merely because they are empty.\n37\t\n38\t## Non-negotiable rules\n39\t\n40\t- **NEVER** use `any`; accept `unknown` and narrow with guards.\n41\t- **NEVER** use non-null assertions (`!`) or type assertions (`as`); narrow or validate.\n42\t- **NEVER** use `@ts-nocheck`, `@ts-ignore`, `@ts-expect-error`, or `eslint-disable`; fix the cause.\n43\t- **NEVER** add an npm package unless the user explicitly requests it; prefer native APIs.\n44\t- **NEVER** remove a symbol to silence lint. Implement it or annotate `// TODO: [Feature] Brief purpose`.\n45\t- **NEVER** undo user edits in `*/types.ts`.\n46\t- **NEVER** write `public`, `protected`, or `private` on a class member, and never declare a parameter property; use runtime-enforced `#` fields.\n47\t- **NEVER** use default exports except where a framework requires them, such as Vue SFCs or config files.\n48\t- **NEVER** use mocks, behavioral fakes, module replacement, framework spies, or fake clocks to simulate project-owned behavior. Use real implementations, recorders, temporary resources, protocol-faithful fixture servers, and inert customizable data stubs.\n49\t- **ALWAYS** make interface properties and public return collections readonly.\n50\t- **ALWAYS** define reusable and public types in `*/types.ts` before implementation.\n51\t- **ALWAYS** inspect the exact declared and installed `@orkestrel/*` capabilities before implementing overlapping logic. Reuse a primitive when its semantics match, and do not wrap it merely to rename it.\n52\t- **ALWAYS** finish the requested implementation: no empty stubs, deferred logic, or concealed follow-up work.\n53\t- **ALWAYS** follow the repository's naming, placement, export, and dependency-direction rules exactly.\n54\t\n55\t## Design laws\n56\t\n57\t- **Types first.** Public contracts precede implementation.\n58\t- **Single-word entity APIs.** Properties, methods, option keys, and events use one descriptive word. If one word is insufficient, change the shape: group options, extract a sub-entity or manager, or split behaviors.\n59\t- **Self-describing helpers.** Module-scope helpers normally use `{verb}{Noun}` because they lack entity context.\n60\t- **One concept, one term.** Do not alternate synonyms. Lifecycle verbs have fixed meanings.\n61\t- **Boolean behavior.** A binary behavioral switch is a boolean, not a two-literal union.\n62\t- **Real domain states only.** Literal unions represent irreducible modes, phases, discriminants, or external values — not decorative labels for facts already represented.\n63\t- **Absence is `undefined`.** Never invent sentinels such as `'none'`, `'unset'`, `'unknown'`, `''`, or `-1`. Use `null` only when an external format distinguishes it from omission.\n64\t- **Derive state.** Compute facts from existing fields. Do not store a second flag or label that can drift.\n65\t- **Named discriminants.** Name the axis that varies (`relationship`, `command`, `category`), never `kind` or `type`.\n66\t- **Centralize by kind.** Types, constants, helpers, validators, parsers, factories, errors, and similar declarations live in their designated centralized files. Implementation files contain one class plus imports.\n67\t- **Export and test reusable logic.** No hidden module helpers or declarations. Fold trivial one-use logic into its caller, or export it from the correct centralized module and test it.\n68\t- **No nested functions.** Extract function declarations and assignments from bodies. The only exceptions are an anonymous callback passed directly as an argument and an anonymous function returned directly as a result.\n69\t- **Functional core, imperative shell.** Export pure leaves. Keep stateful or defining orchestration as class methods. Classes compose behavior; they do not forward 1:1 to helpers.\n70\t- **No superfluous wrappers.** A wrapper must add a boundary, invariant, composition, translation, lifecycle, or materially narrower contract. Otherwise use or rename the real symbol and update every consumer.\n71\t- **Minimal public API.** Add or substantively expand a capability with its first real consumer; do not speculate. This is a creation gate, never a later visibility gate. Once an intentional reusable capability exists, expose its top-level source exports through the correct environment barrel regardless of which consumers currently use them, so developers receive the same supported mechanisms the package uses. Remove a symbol only when the capability itself should not exist. Prefer one minimal interface and one shared engine, allowing native backend overrides only for genuine faster paths.\n72\t- **No compatibility shims.** This is greenfield. Update every consumer in the same change.\n73\t- **Mechanism, not product policy.** Framework code supplies reusable mechanisms and stops before application decisions.\n74\t- **No polling architecture.** Park idle work on events and abort signals. Yield long work cooperatively.\n75\t\n76\t## TTTDD: Types Then Tests Driven Development\n77\t\n78\t1. **Types:** define or revise the contract in `*/types.ts`. Entity-scoped names satisfy the naming rules and properties are readonly.\n79\t2. **Implementation:** conform exactly to the types. Place every declaration in its prescribed file.\n80\t3. **Consolidation:** remove duplication and route repeated behavior through one shared implementation.\n81\t4. **Tests:** cover happy paths, edge cases, failures, and boundary values with targeted deterministic tests.\n82\t5. **Documentation:** update the matching guide or spec and parity coverage.\n83\t\n84\t- Treat a mid-task type change from the user as immediately authoritative. Type failures identify implementation that has not caught up.\n85\t- Insert a failing proof before fixing a defect: record the exact command and its failing count, implement, then record the same command green. A test that never ran red does not bind to the defect it claims.\n86\t- Run the question rather than reasoning about it. Get the smallest real input through the real code and read the real output, as early as the question can be put under a test. Reasoning chooses what to run and interprets what comes back; it never replaces the run.\n87\t- When a claim about a TypeScript edit can name its project, its case, and the edit that must break with the stage it breaks at, call the `prove` tool the `probe` MCP server registers before relying on the claim. `.claude/rules/quality.md` § Instruments owns that rule and the receipt it requires.\n88\t- Test your own assumptions before relying on them, and before stating them. A probe needs no dispute to justify it, and an unverified belief you put into context becomes a fact for everything downstream.\n89\t- Follow the applicable repository skill for comprehensive hardening, research, centralization, contract adoption, real-service integration, or cross-package alignment.\n90\t- Leave no current-scope requirement as a TODO, skipped test, deferred row, or hidden follow-up.\n91\t- Define completion before starting. Enumerate the capabilities the change owns and what closing each one requires. That enumeration is current scope and is fixed when the work begins. Record a finding outside it against the capability that owns it, for the next change, rather than reopening this one.\n92\t\n93\t## Work process\n94\t\n95\t1. **Understand:** clarify scope and entities, and state what completion requires, before starting. Read `*/types.ts`, the rule files, the applicable skill, and the matching guide before editing.\n96\t2. **Research:** when requested or externally material, verify current primary sources and build a capability/defect matrix before changing the API.\n97\t3. **Design:** change types first and typecheck the proposed contract.\n98\t4. **Implement:** match the interface, reuse declared ecosystem primitives, extract centralized logic, and update the sole barrel.\n99\t5. **Consolidate:** remove duplication, nested declarations, and superfluous wrappers without expanding the API.\n100\t6. **Test:** mirror source structure, challenge the applicable seams with real implementations, and run the narrowest relevant project. Reach this step early and often, not once at the end. A question answered by a test is settled; the same question answered in prose is still open.\n101\t7. **Document:** update the guide, examples, and parity contract.\n102\t8. **Verify:** audit discovery, deferrals, and package contents as applicable. Run the required gates and read their actual output before claiming success.\n103\t\n104\tQuality gates before commit, in order. The acceptance gate is the non-mutating variant; run the mutating `lint` and then `format` first only to converge, then prove with the checks. `lint --fix` rewrites code and its output is not formatter-clean, so a `format` that ran before it leaves `format:check` failing on the file `lint` just rewrote:\n105\t\n106\t```text\n107\tnpm run format:check → npm run lint:check → npm run check → npm run build → npm test\n108\t```\n109\t\n110\t- Use scoped checks and tests during development. Do not run the whole suite casually.\n111\t- Type error: read the complete diagnostic, compare implementation with `*/types.ts`, fix one cause, and rerun the relevant check.\n112\t- Unused contract symbol: stop. Implement it or leave the prescribed TODO. Never delete it for lint.\n113\t- Compound entity member: stop. Correct the API shape before implementation. This does not apply to descriptive module helpers.\n114\t- Scope closed and gates green: stop. Report the outcome and move to the next scope. Another pass over the same surface is a new instruction, not diligence.\n115\t\n116\t## Rule map\n117\t\n118\tEvery file in the following table is a normative extension of this root. Read every rule relevant to the files or concepts you touch. Path frontmatter only controls Claude's automatic loading.\n119\t\n120\t| Rule                             | Governs                                                                |\n121\t| -------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n122\t| `.claude/rules/names.md`         | Identifiers, API shape, acronyms, lifecycle vocabulary, files/folders  |\n123\t| `.claude/rules/typescript.md`    | TypeScript syntax, imports, immutability, errors, TSDoc                |\n124\t| `.claude/rules/architecture.md`  | Centralized files, exports, classes, modules, extension points, stores |\n125\t| `.claude/rules/patterns.md`      | Options, managers, emitters, guards, parsers, contracts                |\n126\t| `.claude/rules/tests.md`         | Test behavior, helpers, browser tests, Vitest configuration            |\n127\t| `.claude/rules/workspace.md`     | Src/app environments, aliases, isolation, builds, scripts, tooling     |\n128\t| `.claude/rules/application.md`   | App composition, entries, manifest safety, lifecycle, integration      |\n129\t| `.claude/rules/browser.md`       | Vue/browser architecture and platform usage                            |\n130\t| `.claude/rules/styles.md`        | SCSS/CSS centralization, tokens, mixins, layers, naming                |\n131\t| `.claude/rules/portability.md`   | Host branching, paths, line endings, processes, terminals, OS claims   |\n132\t| `.claude/rules/documentation.md` | Guides, parity, roadmap, showcase, examples                            |\n133\t| `.claude/rules/writing.md`       | Developer prose: reports, replies, vocabulary, claims, structure       |\n134\t| `.claude/rules/quality.md`       | Research, dependency reuse, hardening, completion, package inspection  |\n135\t\n136\t## Documentation contract\n137\t\n138\t- Specs precede code. Read the matching guide, form the intended design, then compare the implementation.\n139\t- Keep public exports and behavioral methods in guide parity. TypeScript, SCSS, Markdown, tests, and showcase must agree.\n140\t- Never suppress a parity failure. Correct the drift.\n141\t\n142\t## Communication\n143\t\n144\t- Do the obvious work without asking for ceremonial permission.\n145\t- Keep chat summaries short. Show exact changes as diffs when useful.\n146\t- Never claim a gate passed until you ran it and read the result.\n147\t\n148\t### Writing\n149\t\n150\tThis governs prose everywhere: chat replies, instruction files, guides, TSDoc, commit messages, and briefs.\n151\t\n152\tFollow ISO 24495-1:2023 Plain Language in every response:\n153\t\n154\t- Give the reader what they need for the task at hand, and nothing else.\n155\t- Put the key point first, and order the rest so the reader finds each part where they look for it.\n156\t- Word every sentence so the reader understands it on the first read.\n157\t- Shape the response so the reader can act on it without asking a follow-up question.\n158\t\n159\tThe rules in this section and `.claude/rules/writing.md` implement these principles. Apply the\n160\tprinciples directly to a case no rule covers.\n161\t\n162\t- Write plainly. Say what you mean; mean what you say.\n163\t- Lead with the decision or the finding. Do not build up to it.\n164\t- One idea per sentence. Keep sentences short.\n165\t- Use the active voice, and the imperative for instructions.\n166\t- State the rule first. Add rationale only when it changes a judgment call, and keep it subordinate.\n167\t- Do not write aphorisms, metaphors, or rhetorical flourish. An aphorism is a memory device for a person; it carries no instruction an agent can act on.\n168\t- Do not use a long or technical word where a short common one works.\n169\t- Keep all substance, nuance, and precision. Cut only what makes text hard to read.\n170\t- Present a tradeoff as option, cost, and recommendation — not as a balanced meditation.\n171\t- Write requirements so they are specific and testable. Replace evaluative words such as \"user friendly\" or \"hardened further\" with the concrete condition that closes them.\n172\t- **NEVER state a count.** A number answering \"how many\" about a set anyone can add to is a count — rules, rows, members, exports, files, options, steps, cases, stages, findings, and tests are such sets. Name the members, or write the sentence without the number.\n173\t- **NEVER name a list item by its position.** Write the item's name, never its ordinal or its number.\n174\t- Treat `both` as a count where it tallies a set that can grow. Keep it where the sentence names the members.\n175\t- Write a number only as a value the reader needs: a duration, a size, a limit, a version, a date, an exit code, or a measurement reported with the run that produced it.\n176\t- Delete a count you find. Do not correct it.\n177\t\n178\t#### Instruction files\n179\t\n180\t`AGENTS.md`, `.claude/rules/*`, `.agents/*`, `.claude/agents/*`, and every skill are executed, not\n181\tread. An agent loads them mid-task and acts on them. Write them for that reader.\n182\t\n183\t- Write every line as a directive: what to do, what to check, or what to refuse. Delete a line that does none of those.\n184\t- Name the observable trigger and the required action. \"When X, do Y\" is actionable; \"X matters\" is not.\n185\t- State the finding as the rule. Never record how it was found, which session found it, what was tried first, or what a probe proved. That history belongs in the commit message.\n186\t- Cut any clause written to persuade, reassure, or explain the rule to a person. An agent needs the rule and its trigger, not agreement with it.\n187\t- Give a rule one home. Restating it elsewhere creates a duplicate that drifts, and an agent reading the stale copy is following this file.\n188\t- Keep an example only when it disambiguates the rule. Delete an example that merely illustrates it.\n189\t"}]},"uuid":"4b770004-5003-4424-a917-0ff34579a617","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:38:22.271Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"aef0d79e-0824-44ad-9c44-224b7883c287","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"0e29d82c-2549-44fa-a43a-4ed484c2cd37","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1243f16d-def8-4fe9-8f90-73e8b336c933","agentId":"a889da7e76aca2c24","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SRXAtvgCakrPK3J2QUfCp6","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# PROPOSAL: scaffold as the single host for the instruction set\n2\t\n3\tStop propagating the instruction files into targets. Scaffold keeps the one canonical\n4\tcopy, every target carries a small pointer, and a session attaches scaffold beside the\n5\trepository it works. Researched and drafted on 2026-08-26; the owner rules on it in a\n6\tscaffold session.\n7\t\n8\t## The problem, measured\n9\t\n10\t- A Claude Code cloud session that attaches the whole fleet injects every checkout's\n11\t  memory set into the model context. The session panel on 2026-08-26 read 335.2k tokens\n12\t  of memory files — 33.5% of a 1M window — across 44 files: eleven repositories times\n13\t  the four files each root auto-loads.\n14\t- The per-repository bytes behind that reading, measured in this checkout: `CLAUDE.md`\n15\t  2,844, `AGENTS.md` 17,950, `.agents/orchestration.md` 63,388, and\n16\t  `.claude/rules/writing.md` 8,220 — roughly 92 KiB of always-loaded prose per\n17\t  repository, byte-identical across all of them.\n18\t- The damage exceeds the visible percentage. A non-fork subagent inherits the full\n19\t  memory hierarchy, so in that session an agent pinned to a smaller-window model failed\n20\t  to boot at all — every launch died on \"Prompt is too long\" before doing any work.\n21\t\n22\t## The harness facts, grounded\n23\t\n24\tEach fact carries its source; \"undocumented\" marks where the documentation is silent and\n25\tthe session must measure.\n26\t\n27\t- Claude Code loads `CLAUDE.md` from the working directory and every ancestor at launch,\n28\t  after managed policy and `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`; subtree copies load on reads into\n29\t  those subtrees (see the memory page, code.claude.com/docs/en/memory).\n30\t- Locally, `--add-dir` directories do **not** load memory by default; the\n31\t  `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1` variable opts in. Cloud multi-repo\n32\t  loading is undocumented, and the observed cloud behavior loads every attached root\n33\t  with no documented off switch.\n34\t- The `claudeMdExcludes` settings key skips memory files by path or glob at any settings\n35\t  layer (memory page). Whether the cloud harness honors it is undocumented.\n36\t- An `@path` import inlines content per importing file — deduplication across importers\n37\t  is undocumented, so assume none — and an import resolving outside the working\n38\t  directory triggers an approval dialog. A pointer written as an import would therefore\n39\t  recreate the duplication or stall on approval; a pointer must be plain prose the agent\n40\t  follows with a Read.\n41\t- Rules without `paths` frontmatter load at launch like `.claude/CLAUDE.md`; rules with\n42\t  `paths` frontmatter load when matching files are read (memory page).\n43\t- `node_modules` is never auto-loaded as memory. A copy there is present on disk, read\n44\t  on demand, and injected never — the right property for a fallback.\n45\t- A non-fork subagent receives every level of the memory hierarchy the main session\n46\t  loads (see the subagents page, code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents).\n47\t\n48\t## The design\n49\t\n50\t- Scaffold's own working tree stays exactly what it is: the canonical `AGENTS.md`,\n51\t  `CLAUDE.md`, `.agents/`, `.claude/`, `.codex/`, `.cursor/`, and `.mcp.json`. Editing\n52\t  them here is the whole change process — no propagation, no fleet churn, no drift, and\n53\t  `scaffold audit` stops policing copies that no longer exist.\n54\t- Every target carries small pointer files in their place. The pointer says, as prose:\n55\t  the coding and orchestration authority is `@orkestrel/scaffold`; when a scaffold\n56\t  checkout sits beside this repository, read `../scaffold/AGENTS.md` and\n57\t  `../scaffold/.agents/orchestration.md` before working; otherwise read the same files\n58\t  from `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/`. Write the pointer pair as two\n59\t  small files (`CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md`) rather than a symlink — the documented\n60\t  `ln -s` trick breaks on Windows checkouts.\n61\t- The Codex and Cursor bridges already instruct by reference — `developer_instructions`\n62\t  in `.codex/config.toml` and the `alwaysApply` rule in `.cursor/rules/` both say \"read\n63\t  `AGENTS.md`, `.agents/orchestration.md`, …\" — so they shrink to the same pointer\n64\t  paths rather than changing shape.\n65\t- A session then attaches scaffold beside the repositories it works. Scaffold's memory\n66\t  loads once; each target contributes a pointer of a few hundred bytes. A whole-fleet\n67\t  session carries one canonical copy instead of eleven.\n68\t- Dispatch briefs name `/home/user/scaffold/AGENTS.md` and its siblings as the files an\n69\t  executor reads before working, which the brief template already parameterizes.\n70\t\n71\t## The migration, mechanically\n72\t\n73\tThe propagation surface is mapped; every pointer below was verified against this\n74\tcheckout on 2026-08-26.\n75\t\n76\t1. Split membership. `HOST_PATHS` (`src/core/constants.ts:124-159`) is one flat list\n77\t   that drives both staging into `dist/host` (`stageHost`,\n78\t   `src/server/helpers.ts:1382-1534`) and restoration into targets (`Materializer`,\n79\t   `src/bin/CLI.ts` `audit`/`repair`). Introduce the distinction the `inferGroup`\n80\t   labels already sketch (`src/core/helpers.ts:216-227`): the instruction members —\n81\t   `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.agents/*`, `.claude/agents`, `.claude/rules`,\n82\t   `.claude/skills`, `.codex/*`, `.cursor/*`, `.mcp.json` — leave the\n83\t   vendored-into-target set; the tool surface — editor and git dotfiles, oxlint,\n84\t   oxfmt, prettier ignore, `configs/`, `scripts/`, the policy tests, `LICENSE`,\n85\t   `guides/guide.md`, `guides/scaffold.md` — stays vendored. Rule during design\n86\t   whether `.claude/settings.json` stays vendored (the permissions floor is tool-read\n87\t   per repository) and whether the instruction files keep shipping in `dist/host` as\n88\t   the `node_modules` fallback while no longer being written into targets — the\n89\t   fallback in the pointer requires that they do.\n90\t2. Add the pointer files to the vendored set, so `repair` installs and polices them:\n91\t   the same destinations (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`), new small content, new digests —\n92\t   `repair` rewrites them in every target as stale entries.\n93\t3. Regenerate. `stageInventory` rewrites `host.json`, and\n94\t   `tests/config.test.ts:594-686` holds committed `host.json` equal to a fresh\n95\t   generation, so the membership change closes there.\n96\t4. Sweep the orphans. `repair` writes only missing and stale entries\n97\t   (`src/server/Materializer.ts:291-314`); a file removed from the inventory is\n98\t   unmanaged, not deleted. Each target needs a one-time deletion of the superseded\n99\t   full copies (`.agents/`, `.claude/agents`, `.claude/rules`, `.claude/skills`,\n100\t   `.codex/`, `.cursor/`, `.mcp.json`) in the same visit that re-pins and repairs.\n101\t5. Re-scope the policy tests. `tests/setupPolicy.ts` inspects the skill family under\n102\t   `.agents/skills` (`:1242-1434`) and the provider bridges under `.claude/skills`\n103\t   (`:1444-1561`); with those trees absent from targets, verify the inspections pass on\n104\t   absence in a target while still binding in scaffold's own tree, and re-scope them\n105\t   where they do not.\n106\t6. Release. The vendored surface moves, so scaffold bumps and publishes on its own\n107\t   account; every target re-pins, runs `repair`, takes the one-time sweep, and proves\n108\t   its gates green.\n109\t\n110\t## Options ruled\n111\t\n112\t- **Scaffold as single host with target pointers** — the preceding design.\n113\t  Recommended: it removes the multiplier, ends propagation churn, and makes scaffold\n114\t  the authority in fact.\n115\t- **`claudeMdExcludes` in session settings** — documented, immediate, no restructuring.\n116\t  It leaves propagation and drift in place, needs per-environment configuration, and\n117\t  its cloud behavior is unverified. Usable as a stopgap while the restructure lands;\n118\t  not the fix.\n119\t- **Slimming the file content** — refused: the multiplier stays, and the churn stays.\n120\t- **Attaching fewer repositories per session** — complementary practice, and no help to\n121\t  a fleet campaign that genuinely needs every checkout.\n122\t\n123\t## Open checks for the executing session\n124\t\n125\t- Measure whether the cloud harness honors `claudeMdExcludes`, and whether it loads\n126\t  `.claude/rules/*.md` per attached root — the documentation is silent on either.\n127\t- Confirm the policy inspections on absent skill trees (step 5).\n128\t- Confirm a cloud session attaching scaffold plus one target loads exactly one\n129\t  canonical copy, by reading the context panel before and after.\n130\t"}]},"uuid":"78a7dfa5-e0a5-41d9-940e-37110b3627d8","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:38:24.486Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0e29d82c-2549-44fa-a43a-4ed484c2cd37","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

Origin says nothing about what scaffold\n16\t * claims at the path; {@link Ownership} says that.\n17\t */\n18\texport type Origin = 'host' | 'template' | 'computed'\n19\t\n20\t/**\n21\t * What scaffold claims at an artifact's path.\n22\t *\n23\t * @remarks\n24\t * `content` claims the bytes: audit compares them, and a write restores a\n25\t * missing file and replaces a stale one. `presence` claims only that the file\n26\t * exists: audit compares existence, and a write restores an absent file and\n27\t * never touches present bytes. `birth` claims only the file's creation: audit\n28\t * never compares it and always reports it aligned, and a write creates it only\n29\t * while it is absent.\n30\t */\n31\texport type Ownership = 'content' | 'presence' | 'birth'\n32\t\n33\t/** The artifact group a plan selects over. */\n34\texport type Group =\n35\t\t| 'manifest'\n36\t\t| 'configs'\n37\t\t| 'source'\n38\t\t| 'tests'\n39\t\t| 'guides'\n40\t\t| 'docs'\n41\t\t| 'orchestration'\n42\t\n43\t/**\n44\t * How one target path compares to the artifact planned for it.\n45\t *\n46\t * @remarks\n47\t * `foreign` is a path the plan does not own at all. It is also the set\n48\t * `overwrite` deletes from, narrowed by the paths no verb may remove and by\n49\t * what git tracks.\n50\t */\n51\texport type Drift = 'aligned' | 'stale' | 'missing' | 'foreign'\n52\t\n53\t/**\n54\t * Whether an upstream lookup produced an answer.\n55\t *\n56\t * @remarks\n57\t * `found` carries the answer. `missing` is an upstream `404`, which is a\n58\t * definite answer that the package is not published there. `unmatched` means\n59\t * the answer was read but no version admits under the declared range. `failed`\n60\t * means the read did not complete because of a transport fault, timeout, byte\n61\t * bound, redirect, or refused response shape. Holding these apart from how a\n62\t * local copy compares is what lets a verdict omit the value it never received\n63\t * instead of inventing an empty one.\n64\t */\n65\texport type Lookup = 'found' | 'missing' | 'unmatched' | 'failed'\n66\t\n67\t/** The compile phases, in the order they run. */\n68\texport type CompileStage = 'draft' | 'gate' | 'pin'\n69\t\n70\t/** The coded reasons a scaffold error is raised. */\n71\texport type ScaffoldErrorCode = 'INVALID' | 'BLOCKED' | 'DESTROYED' | 'TARGET' | 'WRITE' | 'FETCH'\n72\t\n73\t/** The build and export settings one published `src` environment contributes. */\n74\texport interface SrcDefinition {\n75\t\treadonly configs: readonly string[]\n76\t\treadonly project: string\n77\t\treadonly path: string\n78\t\treadonly formats: readonly string[]\n84\t\treadonly project: string\n85\t\treadonly entry?: string\n86\t}\n87\t\n88\t/**\n89\t * Which host-specific pipelines a generated root Vite configuration carries.\n90\t *\n91\t * @remarks\n92\t * Boundary guarantees never vary by blueprint, so they are not selected here:\n93\t * every generated configuration emits the environment-boundary plugin, its\n94\t * module-graph audit, and stylesheet rejection. `browser` selects the shared\n95\t * root CSS analysis and real-browser test machinery. `vue` selects the\n96\t * single-file-component, HTML, and development-server machinery an application\n97\t * browser environment needs. `output` selects build-output containment.\n98\t * `showcase` selects the optional single-file application-browser projection.\n99\t */\n100\texport interface ViteMachinery {\n101\t\treadonly browser: boolean\n102\t\treadonly vue: boolean\n103\t\treadonly output: boolean\n104\t\treadonly showcase: boolean\n105\t}\n106\t\n107\t/**\n108\t * One runtime `@orkestrel/*` dependency of a generated workspace.\n109\t *\n110\t * @remarks\n111\t * `optional` is meaningful only on a blueprint's `peers`, The script\n1465\t * therefore records only the declared inventory and does not invent a service\n1466\t * runner or test project.\n1467\t */\n1468\texport function blueprintToOrchestrationArtifacts(\n1469\t\tblueprint: Blueprint,\n1470\t): readonly ContentArtifact[] {\n1471\t\tif (blueprint.vendors.length === 0) return []\n1472\t\tconst vendors = blueprint.vendors\n1473\t\t\t.map((vendor, index) => `\\t'${vendor}'${index === blueprint.vendors.length - 1 ? '' : ' \\\\'}`)\n1474\t\t\t.join('\\n')\n1475\t\treturn [\n1476\t\t\t{\n1477\t\t\t\tpath: SERVICE_SCRIPT_PATH,\n1478\t\t\t\tgroup: 'orchestration',\n1479\t\t\t\townership: 'birth',\n1480\t\t\t\torigin: 'template',\n1481\t\t\t\tcontent: fillTemplate(ARTIFACT_TEMPLATES.orchestration.service, { vendors }),\n1482\t\t\t},\n1483\t\t]\n1484\t}\n1485\t\n1486\t/**\n1487\t * Compile the vendored host artifacts a named workspace plans.\n1488\t *\n1489\t * @param name - The target workspace's own bare package name.\n1490\t * @returns One artifact per vendored path, in `HOST_PATHS` order.\n1491\t *\n1492\t * @remarks\n1493\t * Every artifact is claimed by presence, which is the strongest claim a pure\n1494\t * compile can make: core cannot read the vendored data root, so it cannot carry\n1495\t * the bytes a content claim would have to be checked against. Reading that root\n1496\t * is what promotes the ones scaffold owns the bytes of.\n1497\t *\n1498\t * `source` is left absent because it falls back to `path`, and every vendored\n1499\t * path is stored under the name it is written to. The group comes from\n1500\t * {@link inferGroup}, so a vendored path and a foreign path found in a target\n1501\t * are classified by one rule and a plan never disagrees with the audit beside\n1502\t * it.\n1503\t *\n1504\t * @example\n1505\t * ```ts\n1506\t * import { nameToHostArtifacts } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n1507\t *\n1508\t * nameToHostArtifacts('router').some((artifact) => artifact.path === 'AGENTS.md') // true\n1509\t * nameToHostArtifacts('router').some((artifact) => artifact.path === 'guides/router.md') // false\n1510\t * ```\n1511\t */\n1512\texport function nameToHostArtifacts(name: string): readonly Artifact[] {\n1513\t\treturn selectHostPaths(HOST_PATHS, name).map((path): Artifact => ({\n1514\t\t\tpath,\n1515\t\t\tgroup: inferGroup(path),\n1516\t\t\townership: 'presence',\n1517\t\t\torigin: 'host',\n1518\t\t}))\n1519\t}\n1520\t\n1521\t/**\n1522\t * Replace the content of every drafted artifact an override names.\n1523\t *\n1524\t * @param artifacts - The drafted artifacts.\n1525\t * @param overrides - The blueprint's overrides.\n1526\t * @returns The artifacts with each matching override applied, in input order.\n1527\t *\n1528\t * @remarks\n1529\t * An override replaces an artifact's whole content and never merges into it.\n1530\t * A host-origin artifact carries no content to replace, so it passes through\n1531\t * untouched; which overrides are legal at all is\n1532\t * {@link overridesToQuestions}'s answer, so an illegal one is reported rather\n1533\t * than silently dropped here.\n1534\t *\n1535\t * @example\n1536\t * ```ts\n1537\t * import { applyOverrides, blueprintToConfigArtifacts, createBlueprint } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n1538\t *\n1539\t * const artifacts = blueprintToConfigArtifacts(createBlueprint('router', { src: ['core'] }))\n1540\t *\n1541\t * applyOverrides(artifacts, [{ path: 'README.md', content: '# Title\\n' }])\n1542\t * ```\n1543\t */\n1544\texport function applyOverrides(\n1545\t\tartifacts: readonly Artifact[],\n1546\t\toverrides: readonly Override[],\n1547\t): readonly Artifact[] {\n1548\t\tif (overrides.length === 0) return artifacts\n1549\t\tconst replacements = new Map(overrides.map((override) => [override.path, override.content]))\n1550\t\treturn artifacts.map((artifact) => {\n1551\t\t\tif (artifact.origin === 'host') return artifact\n1552\t\t\tconst content = replacements.get(artifact.path)\n1553\t\t\treturn content === undefined ? artifact : { ...artifact, content }\n1554\t\t})\n1555\t}\n1556\t\n1557\t/**\n1558\t * Replace declared dependency ranges in package manifest text.\n1559\t *\n1560\t * @param manifest - The manifest text to compile.\n1561\t * @param pins - The runtime and development names and replacement ranges.\n1562\t * @returns The manifest with every matching writable value replaced, or\n1563\t * `undefined` when a name has no quoted declaration in its named section.\n1564\t *\n1565\t * @remarks\n1566\t * The compiler replaces values in place instead of serializing the manifest,\n1567\t * so description, keywords, scripts, key order, indentation, and every byte\n1568\t * outside the named ranges survive. Runtime pins apply only to `dependencies`,\n1569\t * and development pins apply only to `devDependencies`. The compiler never"}]},"uuid":"d9bcdd3e-72dc-4d0a-a6c6-5c67973a5bbc","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:38:32.923Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8039904e-9955-4350-a178-fe7955199ae9","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"af72e922-feb6-4b7b-9634-de354f4001c1","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1243f16d-def8-4fe9-8f90-73e8b336c933","agentId":"a889da7e76aca2c24","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0179gbwAD9YKMV2oRjfesj6P","type":"tool_result","content":"290\t * @remarks\n291\t * `path` is the target-relative path, which is also the checkout-relative path\n292\t * the repository serves, so the file read and the file answered for cannot\n293\t * drift apart. A found lookup carries the file's exact bytes as hexadecimal;\n294\t * one that produced no answer carries the cause and no bytes. `observed` is the target's\n295\t * file as exact bytes when the read was made, and is absent when the target\n296\t * holds no such file; it is the precondition the write is held to, exactly as\n297\t * {@link Finding.observed} is.\n298\t *\n299\t * A found row may carry the target's own bytes rather than read ones, where\n300\t * those bytes already satisfy the claim the read was going to check. They are\n301\t * the same bytes either way, so the row does not record which it holds.\n302\t */\n303\texport type HostFile =\n304\t\t| {\n305\t\t\t\treadonly path: string\n306\t\t\t\treadonly lookup: 'found'\n307\t\t\t\treadonly hex: string\n308\t\t\t\treadonly observed?: string\n309\t\t\t\treadonly note?: never\n310\t\t  }\n311\t\t| {\n312\t\t\t\treadonly path: string\n313\t\t\t\treadonly lookup: 'missing' | 'unmatched' | 'failed'\n314\t\t\t\treadonly note: string\n315\t\t\t\treadonly observed?: string\n316\t\t\t\treadonly hex?: never\n317\t\t  }\n318\t\n319\t/**\n320\t * One dependency guide fetched from upstream, beside the local mirror it answers for.\n321\t *\n322\t * @remarks\n323\t * A found lookup carries the fetched bytes; one that produced no answer carries\n324\t * the cause and no bytes. Either way `observed` is the local mirror's exact\n325\t * bytes as they stood when the fetch was made, and is absent when the mirror\n326\t * was not there; it is the precondition the write is held to, exactly as\n327\t * {@link Finding.observed} is. Whether the mirror is behind is not recorded,\n328\t * because it is `content` against `observed` and a stored answer could only\n329\t * disagree with them. These bytes belong to the catalog verb, which is why a\n330\t * guide mirror is presence-owned: repair restores one that is absent and never\n331\t * replaces one that is present.\n332\t */\n333\texport type Mirror =\n334\t\t| {\n335\t\t\t\treadonly name: string\n336\t\t\t\treadonly path: string\n337\t\t\t\treadonly lookup: 'found'\n338\t\t\t\treadonly content: string\n339\t\t\t\treadonly observed?: string\n340\t\t\t\treadonly note?: never\n341\t\t  }\n342\t\t| {\n343\t\t\t\treadonly name: string\n344\t\t\t\treadonly path: string\n345\t\t\t\treadonly lookup: 'missing' | 'unmatched' | 'failed'\n346\t\t\t\treadonly note: string\n347\t\t\t\treadonly observed?: string\n348\t\t\t\treadonly content?: never\n349\t\t  }\n350\t\n351\t/** The fields every planned file carries. */\n352\texport interface ArtifactBase {\n353\t\treadonly path: string\n354\t\treadonly group: Group\n355\t\treadonly ownership: Ownership\n356\t\treadonly environment?: Environment\n357\t}\n358\t\n359\t/**\n360\t * A file byte-copied from the vendored data root, planned before its bytes are read.\n361\t *\n362\t * @remarks\n363\t * `source` falls back to `path` when absent. The pure core face cannot read the\n364\t * vendored root, so a plan it compiles alone claims only that these files\n365\t * exist. That is why the ownership here is narrowed away from `content`: a\n366\t * claim over bytes nobody has read is a claim that cannot be checked. Reading\n367\t * the vendored root turns the ones scaffold owns the bytes of into\n368\t * {@link HydratedArtifact}. Workspace-owned paths and paths whose bytes belong\n369\t * to another verb stay plain host artifacts, because this writer claims only\n370\t * their presence.\n371\t */\n372\texport interface HostArtifact extends ArtifactBase {\n373\t\treadonly origin: 'host'\n374\t\treadonly ownership: 'presence' | 'birth'\n375\t\treadonly source?: string\n376\t\treadonly hex?: never\n377\t\treadonly content?: never\n378\t}\n379\t\n380\t/**\n381\t * A vendored file whose exact bytes have been read, so its content can be compared.\n382\t *\n383\t * @remarks\n384\t * `hex` is the canonical lowercase byte pairs of the vendored source. It is\n385\t * required, which is what makes content ownership honest: every artifact\n386\t * claiming a byte comparison carries the bytes that comparison needs. Hydration\n387\t * leaves a guide-mirror pointer and the catalog agent as plain host artifacts,\n388\t * because another verb owns those bytes.\n389\t */\n390\texport interface HydratedArtifact extends ArtifactBase {\n391\t\treadonly origin: 'host'\n392\t\treadonly ownership: 'content'\n393\t\treadonly source?: string\n394\t\treadonly hex: string\n395\t\treadonly content?: never\n396\t}\n397\t\n398\t/** A text file produced by the template or computed compilation path. */\n399\texport interface ContentArtifact extends ArtifactBase {\n400\t\treadonly origin: 'template' | 'computed'\n401\t\treadonly content: string\n402\t\treadonly hex?: never\n403\t\treadonly source?: never\n404\t}\n405\t\n406\t/**\n407\t * One file in a plan, discriminated by how its content is produced and what scaffold claims of it.\n408\t *\n409\t * @remarks\n410\t * Every branch that claims `content` ownership carries the bytes to back it:\n411\t * a hydrated artifact through `hex`, a template or computed artifact through\n412\t * `content`. No branch can claim a byte comparison it cannot perform.\n413\t */\n414\texport type Artifact = HostArtifact | HydratedArtifact | ContentArtifact\n415\t\n416\t/** Exact lowercase hexadecimal target bytes keyed by artifact-relative path. */\n417\texport type Snapshot = Readonly<Record<string, string>>\n418\t\n419\t/**\n420\t * The compiled, ordered artifact list and the selection it covers.\n421\t *\n422\t * @remarks\n423\t * `hash` is the plan's content identity and is absent until the pin stage\n424\t * fills it. An artifact at `package.json` carries `birth` ownership because the\n425\t * compiler emits the manifest that way. A plan claiming another ownership at\n426\t * that path contradicts the compiler and is outside this contract.\n427\t */\n428\texport interface Plan {\n429\t\treadonly blueprint: Blueprint\n430\t\treadonly groups: readonly Group[]\n431\t\treadonly artifacts: readonly Artifact[]\n432\t\treadonly hash?: string\n433\t}\n434\t\n435\t/**\n436\t * One drift verdict against a target path.\n437\t *\n438\t * @remarks\n439\t * `observed` carries the destination's exact bytes and is the precondition the\n440\t * mutation is held to: a write that replaces stale bytes and a deletion that\n441\t * removes a foreign file each fail when the destination no longer matches what\n442\t * the finding recorded. Both therefore require it, and the requirement is in\n443\t * the type rather than in prose, because a deletion that cannot bind to what\n444\t * the audit showed is the one thing the destructive verb must never do. A\n445\t * missing destination has no bytes to record. An aligned one may have gone\n446\t * uncompared, which is what a birth-owned path always does, so it records\n447\t * bytes only where they were actually read. Every planned finding carries its\n448\t * artifact's {@link Ownership}, so a consumer reads what scaffold claims at the\n449\t * path from the finding itself. A foreign finding has no planned artifact and\n450\t * therefore no ownership.\n451\t *\n452\t * Which combinations of `ownership`, `drift`, and `observed` a real audit\n453\t * produces is {@link inferDrift}'s law, not this type's. A birth-owned path is\n454\t * never compared and is always aligned, a presence-owned path compares existence\n455\t * only, and bytes are recorded only where they were read. This shape therefore\n456\t * admits a combination that law never produces — a birth-owned path reported\n457\t * stale is the plainest one — and it admits it deliberately: restating the\n458\t * comparison's case analysis here would be a second copy of it, able to disagree\n459\t * with the one that decides. What closes the gap is the writer: `repair` and\n460\t * `remove` re-derive every verdict themselves and refuse a caller's audit that\n461\t * does not match, so a verdict the comparison could not have reached is refused\n462\t * rather than acted on.\n463\t */\n464\texport type Finding =\n465\t\t| {\n466\t\t\t\treadonly path: string\n467\t\t\t\treadonly group: Group\n468\t\t\t\treadonly ownership: Ownership\n469\t\t\t\treadonly drift: 'stale'"}]},"uuid":"cb9739f7-1f29-4d02-85dc-6106e2bf8997","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:38:33.311Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"af72e922-feb6-4b7b-9634-de354f4001c1","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

The\n200\t * manifest files are named exactly; anything\n201\t * {@link matchesOrchestrationPath} accepts is orchestration; `src` and `app`\n202\t * are source; `tests`, `guides`, and `docs` carry their own names; the licence\n203\t * and a root Markdown document are docs; and everything else is configuration.\n204\t * A vendored path and a foreign path found in a target are classified here\n205\t * alike, so the plan and the audit never disagree about what a path is.\n206\t *\n207\t * @example\n208\t * ```ts\n209\t * import { inferGroup } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n210\t *\n211\t * inferGroup('src/core/index.ts') // 'source'\n212\t * inferGroup('AGENTS.md') // 'docs'\n213\t * inferGroup('.editorconfig') // 'configs'\n214\t * ```\n215\t */\n216\texport function inferGroup(path: string): Group {\n217\t\tif (path === 'package.json' || path === 'package-lock.json') return 'manifest'\n218\t\tif (matchesOrchestrationPath(path)) return 'orchestration'\n219\t\tif (path.startsWith('src/') || path.startsWith('app/')) return 'source'\n220\t\tif (path.startsWith('tests/')) return 'tests'\n221\t\tif (path.startsWith('guides/')) return 'guides'\n222\t\tif (path.startsWith('docs/')) return 'docs'\n223\t\t// The licence and the root instruction documents are the workspace's own prose.\n224\t\tif (path === 'LICENSE') return 'docs'\n225\t\tif (!path.includes('/') && path.endsWith('.md')) return 'docs'\n226\t\treturn 'configs'\n227\t}\n228\t\n229\t/**\n230\t * Serialize one string as a single-quoted TypeScript literal.\n231\t *\n232\t * @param value - {"parentUuid":"497e48cd-f240-405c-87a3-c8d2a7019bb6","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1243f16d-def8-4fe9-8f90-73e8b336c933","agentId":"a889da7e76aca2c24","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0169k7eJUa7jCVywZcBkoVW8","type":"tool_result","content":"255\t\t\tif (hash === undefined) {\n256\t\t\t\t// An unidentifiable plan is a question raised against the plan, so the\n257\t\t\t\t// one fail-closed rule still decides it: no plan travels with a blocking\n258\t\t\t\t// question beside it.\n259\t\t\t\tconst refusal: Question = {\n260\t\t\t\t\tfield: 'plan',\n261\t\t\t\t\tmessage: 'The plan carries a value that cannot be encoded, so it has no identity.',\n262\t\t\t\t\tblocking: true,\n263\t\t\t\t}\n264\t\t\t\tstages.push({\n265\t\t\t\t\tstage: 'pin',\n266\t\t\t\t\tinput: drafted,\n267\t\t\t\t\toutput: undefined,\n268\t\t\t\t\tfailure: { code: 'INVALID', message: refusal.message },\n269\t\t\t\t})\n270\t\t\t\treturn { questions: [...questions, refusal], stages }\n271\t\t\t}\n272\t\t\tconst plan: Plan = { ...drafted, hash }\n273\t\t\tstages.push({ stage: 'pin', input: drafted, output: plan })\n274\t\t\treturn { plan, questions, stages }\n275\t\t}\n276\t\n277\t\t// Every artifact the blueprint drafts, in plan order. The manifest is the one artifact this\n278\t\t// package's own fields decide, and it is claimed by birth because a workspace\n279\t\t// owns its manifest after it exists. Every other drafted path is vendored. The\n280\t\t// compile spine selects groups and applies overrides after this full set exists,\n281\t\t// so override legality never depends on the caller's group selection.\n282\t\t#draft(blueprint: Blueprint): readonly Artifact[] {\n283\t\t\treturn [\n284\t\t\t\t{\n285\t\t\t\t\tpath: 'package.json',\n286\t\t\t\t\tgroup: 'manifest',\n287\t\t\t\t\townership: 'birth',\n288\t\t\t\t\torigin: 'computed',\n289\t\t\t\t\tcontent: blueprintToManifest(blueprint),\n290\t\t\t\t},\n291\t\t\t\t...blueprintToConfigArtifacts(blueprint),\n292\t\t\t\t...blueprintToSourceArtifacts(blueprint),\n293\t\t\t\t...blueprintToTestArtifacts(blueprint),\n294\t\t\t\t...blueprintToGuideArtifacts(blueprint),\n295\t\t\t\t...blueprintToDocumentArtifacts(blueprint),\n296\t\t\t\t...blueprintToOrchestrationArtifacts(blueprint),\n297\t\t\t\t...nameToHostArtifacts(blueprint.name),\n298\t\t\t]\n299\t\t}\n300\t\n301\t\t// Blueprint and override laws span the full draft. Artifact bounds measure the\n302\t\t// selected, overridden list the plan will carry.\n303\t\t#gate(\n304\t\t\tblueprint: Blueprint,\n305\t\t\tdraft: readonly Artifact[],\n306\t\t\tartifacts: readonly Artifact[],\n307\t\t): readonly Question[] {\n308\t\t\treturn [\n309\t\t\t\t...blueprintToQuestions(blueprint),\n310\t\t\t\t...overridesToQuestions(blueprint.overrides, draft),\n311\t\t\t\t...artifactsToQuestions(artifacts),\n312\t\t\t]\n313\t\t}\n314\t\n315\t\t// The groups boundary: absence covers every group, a selection is refused\n316\t\t// unless it is one, and what survives is read as membership against `GROUPS`.\n317\t\t#select(groups: readonly Group[] | undefined): readonly Group[] {\n318\t\t\tconst selection =\n319\t\t\t\tgroups === undefined ? undefined : this.#accept(groups, parseGroups, 'groups', GROUPS)\n320\t\t\treturn selectGroups(selection)\n321\t\t}\n322\t\n323\t\t// Snapshot the caller's value, then guard the snapshot. The snapshot is what\n324\t\t// closes the guard/use race: a property backed by an accessor never reaches\n325\t\t// the guard, so what the guard measured is what every later read returns.\n326\t\t#accept<T>(value: unknown, parse: Parser<T>, field: string, candidates?: readonly string[]): T {\n327\t\t\tconst accepted = parse(cloneValue(value))\n328\t\t\tif (accepted !== undefined) return accepted\n329\t\t\tthrow this.#error(\n330\t\t\t\t'INVALID',\n331\t\t\t\t`The ${field} argument is not the exact shape this compiler accepts.`,\n332\t\t\t\tcandidates === undefined ? { field } : { field, candidates },\n333\t\t\t)\n334\t\t}"}]},"uuid":"d9b0363d-f9e1-4d53-97b7-7cf0e2a9e1f3","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:38:52.292Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"497e48cd-f240-405c-87a3-c8d2a7019bb6","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

This file adds only how a Cursor session invokes them.\n14\t\n15\t## Which posture\n16\t\n17\tA Cursor session takes one of these postures. Establish which before acting.\n18\t\n19\t- **Invoked through the `grok` bridge** — a read-only Executor. Return distilled evidence with\n20\t  `file:line` pointers. Never edit, decide, or accept.\n21\t- **Driven directly by the user** — the Orchestrator for this harness, per the orchestration\n22\t  contract.\n23\t\n24\t## Reaching the other engines\n25\t\n26\t- Reach Opus 5 and Sol only through the `codex` and `claude` MCP servers registered in\n27\t  `.cursor/mcp.json`, which run those CLIs on their own accounts.\n28\t- Never select another provider's model from Cursor's own model list. Cursor offers Opus, Sol, and\n29\t  others, but running them here spends Cursor credits for work an MCP server does for free. Cursor\n30\t  is the Grok bench and nothing else.\n31\t- Approve both servers once per machine: `agent mcp enable codex` and `agent mcp enable claude`.\n32\t- MCP serves short interactive exchanges only. {"parentUuid":"79323299-f924-40cf-8bb0-0db0f819ff0f","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1243f16d-def8-4fe9-8f90-73e8b336c933","agentId":"a889da7e76aca2c24","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017rAVUqh9mmdRUiADBspzE2","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t@AGENTS.md\n2\t@.agents/orchestration.md\n3\t\n4\t# Claude Code bridge\n5\t\n6\t`AGENTS.md` governs code. `.agents/orchestration.md` governs agent operation, and every harness\n7\tfollows it. This file adds only what Claude Code does differently, and cannot weaken either.\n8\t\n9\t## Dispatch mechanism\n10\t\n11\t- Use the Agent tool for a single dispatch, including when later control flow depends on its result.\n12\t- Use a Workflow for a deterministic fan-out, staged pipeline, or loop. Serialize writing nodes.\n13\t- Recover an interrupted Workflow with `resumeFromRunId`.\n14\t- Foreground Bash is hard-capped at 10 minutes regardless of its timeout parameter. Launch anything\n15\t  that can exceed it as a harness-tracked background command.\n16\t- Never dispatch an adversarial lane with a context-inheriting subagent type such as `fork`. A lane\n17\t  must start clean, or it inherits the Orchestrator's framing and stops being independent.\n18\t\n19\t## Models\n20\t\n21\t- Use the aliases `opus` and `sonnet`. Never use a fixed Claude model ID and never use `inherit`.\n22\t- Never set `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL`. It flattens the engine split.\n23\t- Run the main session on `opus` at high effort, set by `/model opus` or `\"model\": \"opus\"`. Opus 5\n24\t  is the Orchestrator in this harness. Its Orchestrator duties are unchanged if it is configured\n25\t  otherwise.\n26\t- The Orchestrator shares its engine with `planner`, `reviewer`, and the Opus `implementer`. Run\n27\t  the Sol `analyst` in every design round and every audit round so the judgment is not single-engine,\n28\t  and confirm from its journal that it reached Sol. A bridge driver that answers from its own engine\n29\t  collapses the round to one engine and its ruling still reads as normal.\n30\t- Claude role frontmatter accepts Claude models only. Reach Grok through `grok`, and Sol through\n31\t  `analyst` and `sol`. Never put an external model in `model:`. Treat\n32\t  `.agents/transports/codex.md` as the transport contract for those bridges, never as a\n33\t  dispatchable role.\n34\t- Claude Code hot-reloads edits to existing role files.\n35\t\n36\t## Bench wiring\n37\t\n38\t- `.mcp.json` registers `codex mcp-server` for short interactive exchanges with Sol. Project MCP\n39\t  servers are enabled without prompting, so the wiring works headless.\n40\t- `.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` is a bridge that loads the canonical skill from\n41\t  `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`. It adds no independent process.\n42\t- Claude Code exposes `claude mcp serve`, which is how a Codex-primary session reaches Opus 5.\n43\t\n44\t## Claude Code Cloud\n45\t\n46\t- Cloud setup installs `@openai/codex` globally and never authenticates. {"parentUuid":"008b293c-aa71-4b24-bab6-c2219759ecfb","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1243f16d-def8-4fe9-8f90-73e8b336c933","agentId":"a889da7e76aca2c24","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QdWaasYmBJ3deJro2kZNLV","type":"tool_result","content":"1085\t/**\n1086\t * The vendored paths that are directories rather than files.\n1087\t *\n1088\t * @remarks\n1089\t * `HOST_PATHS` mixes files and directories and says which is which nowhere, because the\n1090\t * vendored root's own manifest is what decides it. A fixture builds that\n1091\t * manifest, so the fixture declares the split. `.claude/skills` is the one\n1092\t * declared directory no entry sits beneath, which makes it the empty-directory\n1093\t * case every writer has to survive.\n1094\t */\n1095\texport const HOST_DIRECTORY_PATHS: readonly string[] = [\n1096\t\t'.agents/skills',\n1097\t\t'.claude/agents',\n1098\t\t'.claude/rules',\n1099\t\t'.claude/skills',\n1100\t\t'.codex/agents',\n1101\t\t'.cursor/rules',\n1102\t]\n1103\t\n1104\t/**\n1105\t * Build the manifest a vendored root storing every planned path declares.\n1106\t *\n1107\t * @returns A manifest whose digest is computed from the membership it carries.\n1108\t *\n1109\t * @remarks\n1110\t * Wider than {@link buildVendoredManifest}, and for a different question: that\n1111\t * one gives a writer every shape a vendored entry has, while this one gives a\n1112\t * whole compiled plan somewhere to resolve. A plan claims every path in\n1113\t * `HOST_PATHS`, and a host that does not carry one of them refuses the write, so\n1114\t * a fixture driving the executable needs the complete set rather than a sample.\n1115\t * Each directory except the declared empty one is given one file, which is what\n1116\t * makes its expansion observable.\n1117\t */\n1118\texport function buildFleetManifest(): HostManifest {\n1119\t\tconst entries: ManifestEntry[] = []\n1120\t\tconst roots: string[] = []\n1121\t\tfor (const path of HOST_PATHS) {\n1122\t\t\tif (!HOST_DIRECTORY_PATHS.includes(path)) {\n1123\t\t\t\tentries.push(buildManifestEntry({ storage: pathToStorage(path), destination: path }))\n1124\t\t\t\tcontinue\n1125\t\t\t}\n1126\t\t\troots.push(path)\n1127\t\t\tif (path === '.claude/skills') continue\n1128\t\t\tconst destination = path === '.claude/agents' ? CATALOG_AGENT_PATH : `${path}/sample.md`\n1129\t\t\tentries.push(buildManifestEntry({ storage: pathToStorage(destination), destination }))\n1130\t\t}\n1131\t\tconst membership = { entries, roots: [...roots].sort() }\n1132\t\treturn { ...membership, digest: computeManifestDigest(membership.entries, membership.roots) }\n1133\t}\n1134\t\n1135\t/**\n1136\t * Write a real checkout carrying every vendored path, as the stager reads one.\n1137\t *\n1138\t * @param workspace - The temporary workspace the checkout is written into.\n1139\t * @param relative - The workspace-relative directory to write it at.\n1140\t * @returns The checkout's absolute path.\n1141\t *\n1142\t * @remarks\n1143\t * The stager reads `HOST_PATHS` out of core, which no test can vary, so the\n1144\t * checkout beneath it is the only seam a stager test has. Every file carries its\n1145\t * own path as its content, so a file staged under the wrong storage name is\n1146\t * visible in the assertion rather than in a count. Each vendored directory is\n1147\t * given one file except `.claude/skills`, which is left genuinely empty because\n1148\t * that is the root a file inventory cannot see and the one the manifest exists\n1149\t * to declare.\n1150\t */\n1151\texport function createCheckout(workspace: ScratchInterface, relative: string): string {\n1152\t\tconst root = workspace.ensure(relative)\n1153\t\tfor (const path of HOST_PATHS) {\n1154\t\t\tif (!HOST_DIRECTORY_PATHS.includes(path)) {\n1155\t\t\t\tworkspace.write(`${relative}/${path}`, `${path}\\n`)\n1156\t\t\t\tcontinue\n1157\t\t\t}\n1158\t\t\tworkspace.ensure(`${relative}/${path}`)\n1159\t\t\tif (path === '.claude/skills') continue\n1160\t\t\tworkspace.write(`${relative}/${path}/sample.md`, `${path}/sample.md\\n`)\n1161\t\t}\n1162\t\treturn root\n1163\t}\n1164\t\n1165\t/**\n1166\t * Build the manifest a host staged from {@link createCheckout} must declare.\n1167\t *\n1168\t * @returns A manifest whose digest is computed from the membership it carries.\n1169\t *\n1170\t * @remarks\n1171\t * Written from the checkout the fixture wrote rather than from anything the\n1172\t * stager returns, so a stager that vendored the wrong set, mapped a storage name\n1173\t * differently, or dropped the empty root is caught by an unequal membership. The\n1174\t * order is stated too, because the digest authenticates order: entries sort by\n1175\t * storage name and roots sort as paths.\n1176\t */\n1177\texport function buildCheckoutManifest(): HostManifest {\n1178\t\tconst entries: ManifestEntry[] = []\n1179\t\tconst roots: string[] = []\n1180\t\tfor (const path of HOST_PATHS) {\n1181\t\t\tif (!HOST_DIRECTORY_PATHS.includes(path)) {\n1182\t\t\t\tentries.push(\n1183\t\t\t\t\tbuildManifestEntry({\n1184\t\t\t\t\t\tstorage: pathToStorage(path),\n1185\t\t\t\t\t\tdestination: path,\n1186\t\t\t\t\t\texecutable: matchesExecutablePath(path),\n1187\t\t\t\t\t\tdigest: computeDigest(`${path}\\n`),\n1188\t\t\t\t\t}),\n1189\t\t\t\t)\n1190\t\t\t\tcontinue\n1191\t\t\t}\n1192\t\t\troots.push(path)\n1193\t\t\tif (path === '.claude/skills') continue\n1194\t\t\tconst destination = `${path}/sample.md`"}]},"uuid":"cedc0d0a-7532-4420-8503-4ebc054c7800","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:39:48.901Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"008b293c-aa71-4b24-bab6-c2219759ecfb","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

[]\n304\t\t\tconst preconditions: WritePrecondition[] = []\n305\t\t\tfor (const artifact of hydrated.artifacts) {\n306\t\t\t\tconst drift = drifted.get(artifact.path)\n307\t\t\t\tif (drift !== guides to their local mirrors.\n319\t\t *\n320\t\t * @param mirrors - The fetched guides; each carries the local bytes its write is held to.\n321\t\t * @param target - The directory to write into.\n322\t\t * @returns The mirror paths written and skipped; a mirror already current is skipped.\n323\t\t * @throws {@link ScaffoldError} coded `INVALID` when an argument is not the\n324\t\t * exact shape, `TARGET` when a mirror moved since it was fetched, `WRITE` when\n325\t\t * the write cannot be staged or committed, and `DESTROYED` after teardown.\n326\t\t *\n327\t\t * @remarks\n328\t\t * A verdict carrying no bytes carries a cause instead, so it is skipped rather\n329\t\t * than written: one unreachable package never costs the caller the rest of the\n330\t\t * fetch, and it never empties a mirror it could not replace.\n331\t\t */\n332\t\tmirror(mirrors: readonly Mirror[], target: string): MaterializeResult {\n333\t\t\tthis.#assertAlive()\n334\t\t\tconst accepted = this.#accept(mirrors, isMirrors, 'mirrors')\n335\t\t\tconst directory = this.#accept(target, isFilesystemPath, 'target')\n336\t\t\tconst writes: Artifact[] = []\n337\t\t\tconst skipped: string[] = []\n338\t\t\tconst preconditions: WritePrecondition[] = []\n339\t\t\tfor (const fetched of accepted) {\n340\t\t\t\tif (fetched.lookup !== 'found' || fetched.observed === contentToHex(fetched.content)) {\n341\t\t\t\t\tskipped.push(fetched.path)\n342\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n343\t\t\t\t}\n344\t\t\t\tconst current = readFileHex(directory, fetched.path)\n345\t\t\t\tif (current !== fetched.observed) {\n346\t\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', `The mirror at ${fetched.path} moved since it was fetched.`, {\n347\t\t\t\t\t\ttarget: directory,\n348\t\t\t\t\t\tpath: fetched.path,\n349\t\t\t\t\t})\n350\t\t\t\t}\n351\t\t\t\tpreconditions.push(this.#bind(directory, fetched.path, current === undefined))\n352\t\t\t\twrites.push({\n353\t\t\t\t\tpath: fetched.path,\n354\t\t\t\t\tgroup: inferGroup(fetched.path),\n355\t\t\t\t\townership: 'content',\n356\t\t\t\t\torigin: 'computed',\n357\t\t\t\t\tcontent: fetched.content,\n358\t\t\t\t})\n359\t\t\t}\n360\t\t\treturn this.#apply(directory, writes, [], skipped, preconditions)\n361\t\t}\n362\t\n363\t\t/**\n364\t\t * Rewrite the marker-bounded package table in the target's catalog agent file.\n365\t\t *\n366\t\t * @param entries - The published packages the table must list.\n367\t\t * @param target - The directory to write into.\n368\t\t * @returns The catalog path, written when the region's bytes moved and skipped otherwise.\n369\t\t * @throws {@link ScaffoldError} coded `INVALID` when an argument is not the\n370\t\t * exact shape, `TARGET` when the file is unreadable or carries no marked\n371\t\t * region, `WRITE` when the write cannot be staged or committed, and\n372\t\t * `DESTROYED` after teardown.\n373\t\t *\n374\t\t * @remarks\n375\t\t * Only the text between the markers is replaced, so every word a consumer\n376\t\t * wrote around the table survives the call. A row whose lookup produced no\n377\t\t * version prints the cause it carries instead, because dropping the row would\n378\t\t * hide a package the organization publishes behind one failed request.\n379\t\t */\n380\t\tcatalog(entries: readonly CatalogEntry[], target: string): MaterializeResult {\n381\t\t\tthis.#assertAlive()\n382\t\t\tconst accepted = this.#accept(entries, isCatalogEntries, 'entries')\n383\t\t\tconst directory = this.#accept(target, isFilesystemPath, 'target')\n384\t\t\treturn this.#rewrite(\n385\t\t\t\tdirectory,\n386\t\t\t\tCATALOG_AGENT_PATH,\n387\t\t\t\tMAX_ARTIFACT_BYTES,\n388\t\t\t\tthis.#recatalog(accepted),\n389\t\t\t)\n390\t\t}\n391\t\n392\t\t/**\n393\t\t * Rewrite the manifest regions the caller names in the target's manifest.\n394\t\t *"}]},"uuid":"03e993e3-2fc5-4a95-a7f6-e977a62e6a1d","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:40:39.205Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7ea65bbf-8e95-450c-a16c-6381d0ed1cc6","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"b1a671c5-693b-4b52-8d0a-3af6f8703646","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"1243f16d-def8-4fe9-8f90-73e8b336c933","agentId":"a889da7e76aca2c24","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01K7iCLFxsY4CADCN9EXt8YT","type":"tool_result","content":"190\t}\n191\t\n192\t/**\n193\t * Project a target-relative path to the storage name a vendored host holds it under.\n194\t *\n195\t * @param path - The target-relative path the file is written to.\n196\t * @returns The storage name beneath the host root.\n197\t *\n198\t * @remarks\n199\t * A staged host is a plain directory that npm packs, and npm's own ignore rules\n200\t * would drop a leading-dot entry from the tarball. So every dot that opens a\n201\t * segment comes off, and a dotted file at the root moves under `dotfiles/` to\n202\t * keep it from colliding with an undotted sibling of the same name. The mapping\n203\t * is one direction only: a staged host's manifest records the destination each\n204\t * storage name answers for, so the reader never re-derives this.\n205\t *\n206\t * @example\n207\t * ```ts\n208\t * import { pathToStorage } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n209\t *\n210\t * pathToStorage('.gitignore') // 'dotfiles/gitignore'\n211\t * pathToStorage('.claude/rules/names.md') // 'claude/rules/names.md'\n212\t * pathToStorage('AGENTS.md') // 'AGENTS.md'\n213\t * ```\n214\t */\n215\texport function pathToStorage(path: string): string {\n216\t\tconst segments = path.split('/')\n217\t\tif (segments.length === 1) {\n218\t\t\treturn path.startsWith('.') ? `dotfiles/${path.slice(1)}` : path\n219\t\t}\n220\t\treturn segments.map((segment) => (segment.startsWith('.') ? segment.slice(1) : segment)).join('/')\n221\t}\n222\t\n223\t/**\n224\t * Compute the SHA-256 digest of text.\n225\t *\n226\t * @param content - The text to digest.\n227\t * @returns Sixty-four lowercase hexadecimal digits.\n228\t *\n229\t * @remarks"}]},"uuid":"936c9192-d5eb-4deb-9200-17a727971148","timestamp":"2026-08-27T01:42:23.966Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b1a671c5-693b-4b52-8d0a-3af6f8703646","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}
