The pointer pair ships as a template artifact, not as a vendored file.\n\nThat inversion is the whole design. `PROPOSAL.md:76-95` reads as though membership is one thing; the code says it is two things sharing one list (`stageHost` walks `HOST_PATHS` at `src/server/helpers.ts:1401`; `nameToHostArtifacts` selects from it at `src/core/compilers.ts:1513`). Separating those two readings costs one constant and one predicate, and it makes the `node_modules` fallback free rather than a thing to arrange.\n\n## The discriminant\n\nThe axis is not \"instruction versus tool\". It is **what a harness loads before the work asks for it** versus **what a program reads on demand**.\n\nThat axis explains every edge case without further argument:\n\n- `.claude/rules/writing.md` loads at launch in every attached root. It is the multiplier. It leaves.\n- `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md` loads when a role is dispatched. It costs nothing ambient, and a target-rooted session cannot dispatch a role whose file is not in that root. It stays.\n- `.claude/settings.json` never enters a model context at all. Removing it buys zero tokens and dismantles the permission floor and the `SessionStart` hooks that run `scripts/*.sh`. It stays.\n\nCall the leaving set **ambient**: loaded whether or not the work needs it.\n\n## 1. The membership split\n\n**Constants** (`src/core/constants.ts`):\n\n- `HOST_PATHS` — membership unchanged. Its TSDoc is rewritten to say what it now means: the paths a staged host **carries**. It drives `stageHost`, `stageInventory`, `dist/host`, and `host.json`, and nothing else.\n- `AMBIENT_PATHS: readonly string[]` — new, frozen. The `HOST_PATHS` members every harness loads into an agent's context before the work names them, and which a target therefore references instead of copying. Members: `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.agents/orchestration.md`, `.agents/skills`, `.claude/rules`, `.claude/skills`.\n\n`{QUALIFIER}_{NOUN}`, `Object.freeze`, `readonly string[]` — same shape as `EXECUTABLE_PATHS` and `WORKSPACE_OWNED_PATHS` beside it.\n\n**Helpers** (`src/core/helpers.ts`):\n\n- `matchesAmbientPath(path: string): boolean` — true for a member or anything beneath a directory member. It mirrors `matchesOrchestrationPath` exactly, which is the family a reader already knows.\n- `selectHostPaths(paths, name)` — same signature, widened contract: drops the workspace's own guide **and** every ambient path. Every call site is unchanged. This is where \"derive state\" is honoured: there is no second membership list to drift, only a declared subset and a filter.\n\n**Types: none change.** No new `Group` member, no new artifact branch, no new discriminant field. The split is set membership over the existing model, so the named-discriminant pressure never arises — a predicate is the right shape for a predicate. `HostArtifact`, `HydratedArtifact`, `ContentArtifact`, `Plan`, `Finding`, `HostManifest`, and `ManifestEntry` are untouched.\n\n**What follows without further work:**\n\n- `stageHost` and `stageInventory` are unedited, so `host.json` and `dist/host` are byte-identical and `tests/config.test.ts:594-694` stays green with no regeneration. `PROPOSAL.md:93-95` expects a regeneration; under this design there is none to do, and that is a proof rather than a gap.\n- The `node_modules` fallback the pointer names exists automatically, because the instruction files never left the staged set.\n- `Materializer.#expand` (`src/server/Materializer.ts:658-687`) refuses a planned path the manifest lacks. The plan only shrinks, so that refusal is never reached. Manifest entries with no planned artifact are simply never expanded.\n- `tests/setupServer.ts` `HOST_DIRECTORY_PATHS`, `createCheckout`, `buildFleetManifest`, and the packed-path expansion in `tests/distribution.test.ts:250-287` all key off `HOST_PATHS` and need no edit.\n\n**One correction the split forces.** `src/bin/CLI.ts:632-635` builds the online overlay path list from every manifest entry. After the split that fetches the whole rule family, every skill, and `.agents/orchestration.md` from upstream on each online `audit`, for paths no target plans — roughly 92 KiB of pointless traffic per run, and a `readSnapshot` over files the target no longer holds. Add `matchesAmbientPath` to that filter beside `isDeferredPath`.\n\n## 2. Pointer mechanism and content\n\n**Mechanism: a template artifact, not a vendored member.**\n\n`ARTIFACT_TEMPLATES.docs` gains `agents` and `claude` beside `readme` (`src/core/templates.ts:2010`). `blueprintToDocumentArtifacts` returns the pointer pair alongside `README.md`:\n\n- `path: 'AGENTS.md'` / `path: 'CLAUDE.md'`\n- `group: 'docs'` (what `inferGroup` already returns for both)\n- `origin: 'template'`\n- `ownership: 'content'` — scaffold owns the pointer bytes, so `repair` restores a missing one and replaces a stale one, which is exactly the behaviour `PROPOSAL.md:90-92` asks for. `README.md` keeps `birth`; the difference is deliberate and readable.\n\nNo new compiler, no new barrel row, no new constant for the destinations — the same inline-literal form `README.md` already uses.\n\nVendoring the pointer instead is not merely more work; `stageHost:1454-1460` refuses two vendored paths claiming one storage name, and scaffold's own `AGENTS.md` already claims `AGENTS.md`. A vendored pointer needs `HOST_PATHS` entries to become objects carrying `source`, which ends the frozen string list and rewrites every consumer of it. The template path costs one table entry.\n\n**Content: the fallback paths in `PROPOSAL.md:58` are wrong, and the design fixes them.**\n\n`pathToStorage` (`src/server/helpers.ts:215-221`) strips the leading dot from each segment and moves a root dotted file under `dotfiles/`. So under `dist/host` the files sit at:\n\n| Destination | Storage under `dist/host` |\n| --- | --- |\n| `AGENTS.md` | `AGENTS.md` |\n| `.agents/orchestration.md` | `agents/orchestration.md` |\n| `.claude/rules/names.md` | `claude/rules/names.md` |\n| `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` | `agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` |\n\nA pointer that names `dist/host/.agents/orchestration.md` sends every agent to a path that does not exist. The pointer names the storage paths.\n\n**The `AGENTS.md` pointer body:**\n\n```markdown\n# AGENTS.md\n\nThe `@orkestrel/scaffold` package owns this repository's coding contract and its\norchestration contract. This file carries no rules of its own, and an edit here is\nreverted the next time `scaffold repair` runs.\n\nRead the following files before working, and follow them as though they sat in this\nrepository:\n\n- The coding contract, `AGENTS.md`.\n- The agent operation contract, `.agents/orchestration.md`.\n- Every rule that contract's Rule map names for the files you touch, under `.claude/rules/`.\n- The dispatch-named skill and the references it names, under `.agents/skills/`.\n\nRead them from the scaffold checkout beside this repository when one is present:\n`../scaffold/AGENTS.md`, `../scaffold/.agents/orchestration.md`, `../scaffold/.claude/rules/`,\n`../scaffold/.agents/skills/`.\n\nOtherwise read them from the installed package, where a leading dot is stripped from\neach path segment: `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/AGENTS.md`,\n`node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/agents/orchestration.md`,\n`node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/claude/rules/`,\n`node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/agents/skills/`.\n\nChange any of them in the scaffold checkout. That edit reaches this repository through a\n`@orkestrel/scaffold` release, a re-pin, and `scaffold repair`.\n```\n\nThe `CLAUDE.md` pointer is the same shape with a Claude Code opening line and no rule-map paragraph, so a Claude session that loads only `CLAUDE.md` still reaches everything.\n\nThree constraints the prose obeys, each checkable:\n\n- **Every `@` sits inside backticks.** Claude Code ignores an import inside a code span, and a bare `@orkestrel/scaffold` at the start of a line is an import that either inlines the package or raises an approval dialog (`PROPOSAL.md:36-40`). This is the failure the whole design exists to avoid, so it is an acceptance criterion, not a style note.\n- **Imperative, present tense, `you` for the reader, no `should`, no `simply`, no count.** `.claude/rules/writing.md` binds the pointer the same as any guide.\n- **The condition precedes the instruction**: \"when a scaffold checkout sits beside this repository, read …\". A reader the branch does not apply to stops at the first clause.\n\n## 3. `.claude/settings.json`\n\n**Stays vendored.** Four reasons, any one sufficient:\n\n- It is never loaded as memory. Removing it saves nothing, and the change's entire justification is tokens.\n- It is the permissions floor. A floor that is not in the target's own root is not a floor; `--add-dir` imports no settings.\n- Its `SessionStart` hooks invoke `scripts/deps.sh`, `scripts/ollama.sh`, `scripts/cursor.sh`, and `scripts/codex.sh` (`.claude/settings.json:968-995`), which stay vendored. Splitting the pair leaves hooks with no runner.\n- It keeps a target reaching the `{.agents,.claude,.codex,.cursor,.github}/**/*` branch of `POLICY_PORTABILITY_GLOB`, which is what lets the reach assertion in the policy suite stay honest without a scaffold-only branch.\n\n## 4. Bridge files in targets\n\nA target keeps the bridge **wiring** and loses the instruction **bodies**.\n\n| Path | Ruling | Why |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `.mcp.json` | Stays | The harness reads it to register MCP servers. A target with no `.mcp.json` has no `probe` and no Sol bridge. |\n| `.cursor/mcp.json` | Stays | Same, for Cursor. |\n| `.codex/config.toml` | Stays, prose rewritten | The Codex CLI reads configuration from the repository it runs in. Its `developer_instructions` block is small and Claude never loads it, so it is not part of the multiplier. Rewrite that block to the pointer paths. |\n| `.cursor/rules/orchestration.mdc` | Stays, prose rewritten | `alwaysApply: true` injects it, but it is a few hundred bytes and Cursor-only. Rewrite its opening paragraph to the pointer paths. |\n| `.claude/agents/**` | Stays | Role files resolve from the session's own root. A target that loses them cannot dispatch `planner`, `analyst`, `grok`, or `verifier`, which makes the orchestration contract unexecutable in that repository. Bodies load on dispatch, not at launch. |\n| `.codex/agents/**` | Stays | Same, for Codex. |\n\nA Codex or Cursor session on a bare target therefore reads its own bridge, which names `../scaffold/AGENTS.md` and the `dist/host` fallback, and follows it.\n\n**`.claude/skills` leaves, paired with `.agents/skills`, and the law decides it rather than taste.** `inspectSkillBridges` (`tests/setupPolicy.ts:1592-1623`) fails on a twin-directory violation when bridges exist and the canonical family does not. Keeping the bridges while the canonical bodies leave turns the vendored policy suite red in every target. Both empty passes; either alone fails. The pair leaves.\n\nCost, named honestly: a target session cannot invoke a skill by its bare name. The Orchestrator names the skill's resolved path in the brief, which the dispatch contract already requires it to do.\n\n## 5. The catalog agent\n\n**It does not move, and nothing about it changes.** `.claude/agents` stays vendored, so `CATALOG_AGENT_PATH` still resolves, `isDeferredPath` (`src/core/helpers.ts:188-189`) still defers it, hydration still leaves it presence-owned, and `Materializer.catalog` still rewrites the marked region. This question dissolves once role files stay, which is the strongest argument that the ambient discriminant is drawn in the right place.\n\n## 6. Policy-test re-scope\n\nTwo assertions fail in a bare target, and each fails for a different reason. Fix each at its own cause; add no scaffold-only branch inside a fleet-shared file.\n\n**`readPolicyPaths` reach (`tests/policy.test.ts:493-499`).** The assertion's subject is that the glob population reaches each of its branches. `.claude/rules/names.md` is one witness for the harness-directory branch; `.claude/settings.json` is another, and it exists in every workspace including scaffold. Swap the witness. Nothing weakens: the branch is still proven non-empty, by a member every target actually has.\n\n**Skill family non-empty containing `orkestrel-falsify` (`tests/policy.test.ts:348-352`).** This is not fleet law. It is a claim about scaffold's own repository, sitting in a file byte-identical across eleven repositories — miswritten before this change and merely exposed by it. Delete it from the vendored file. The vendored file keeps `inspectSkillFamily(cwd)` and `inspectSkillBridges(cwd)` equal to `[]`, which bind wherever the trees exist and pass on absence, which is correct.\n\nRe-home the claim in scaffold's own, non-vendored, always-run proof: assert the committed `host.json` carries entries at storage `agents/skills/orkestrel-falsify/SKILL.md` and `claude/rules/names.md`. That runs in the `src:server` project on every `npm test`, and it proves more than the original — that the canonical family exists **and** that the published fallback the pointer sends agents to actually delivers it. `tests/config.test.ts` already proves `host.json` equals a fresh stage, so the chain closes.\n\nAdd one positive fixture case to the vendored file in the existing `inspectPolicyControl` style: a fixture root shaped like a post-migration target — pointer `AGENTS.md`, `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/agents/`, no `.agents/skills`, no `.claude/rules` — reporting no violations. That is the assertion that would have caught this class, and it belongs where the law lives.\n\n`inspectPolicyRules` needs no change: `tests/setupPolicy.ts:1660` returns `[]` when no rule file exists, so the rule-map law passes vacuously in a target whose pointer `AGENTS.md` carries no Rule map.\n\n## 7. The orphan sweep\n\n**Scaffold ships detection, never deletion. The visit performs the removal.**\n\n`Materializer.repair` writes only `missing` and `stale` and never deletes (`src/server/Materializer.ts:305-313`). `overwrite` deletes tracked foreign findings, but a foreign finding requires an owned root, and `#roots(plan)` (`:639-654`) derives owned roots from planned host artifacts. Once `.claude/rules` leaves the plan it is not an owned root, so its leftovers are invisible to `overwrite` too. The superseded copies are unreachable by every existing verb, by construction.\n\nAdding a verb that deletes directories scaffold does not claim would be product policy dressed as mechanism, and it would be permanently dangerous for the sake of one visit. Leaving the copies silently in place is worse: the multiplier survives and the change buys nothing.\n\nThe seam already exists. `audit` raises target questions about facts scaffold does not plan — `#setupQuestion` (`src/bin/CLI.ts:1359`) is precisely that shape, and its own comment states the posture: raised by `audit` alone, naming nothing scaffold plans, never blocking a write. Add `#supersededQuestion(target)`: it names every `AMBIENT_PATHS` member present in the target and prescribes the removal. It is advisory, non-destructive, self-extinguishing after the sweep, and it costs no new capability.\n\nThe visit's removal is `git rm -r` of the named paths, and git is the undo.\n\n## 8. Membership edges\n\n| Path | Ruling | Reason |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `scripts/*.sh` | Tool surface, stays | Run by the target's own `SessionStart` hooks. `EXECUTABLE_PATHS` unchanged. |\n| `.agents/templates/**` | Stays | Read when a brief is written, not at launch. Same class as a role file. |\n| `.agents/transports/**` | Stays | Role files that stay bind these contracts by reference. Letting them leave would force a rewrite of every `.claude/agents/*.md` and `.codex/agents/*.md` body to carry a pointer-resolved path, for a few KiB that never enter a context. |\n| `LICENSE` | Stays | Legal text a target must carry as its own file. |\n| `guides/guide.md` | Stays | The `@orkestrel/guide` contract a generated workspace starts from. Unrelated to instruction loading. |\n| `guides/scaffold.md` | Stays | The mirror a target holds of scaffold's own guide; `selectHostPaths` already drops it in scaffold itself. |\n\n`.agents/` therefore splits: `orchestration.md` and `skills/` leave, `templates/` and `transports/` stay. That is uncomfortable to look at and correct by the discriminant, and it is the tension I most want the other lane to attack.\n\n## 9. Documentation parity\n\n| File | What the change owns |\n| --- | --- |\n| `guides/scaffold.md` | Surface-table rows for `AMBIENT_PATHS` and `matchesAmbientPath`. Groups section (`885-898`): `docs` now names the pointer pair rather than vendored instruction documents. Ownership section (`900-949`): the pointer pair is template-origin and content-owned; `AGENTS.md` is no longer a vendored path. Vendored data root (`1127-1183`): state the two readings of membership — what a host **carries** versus what a target **plans** — and name the storage mapping the pointer relies on. A new section transcribing both pointer bodies, which `tests/guides.test.ts` executes. |\n| `README.md:6-8,36-57` | Rewrite. Scaffold ships the shared **tool** set as data every target carries, and the shared **instruction** set as a published surface every target points at. `new`, `audit`, and `repair` descriptions follow. |\n| `ROADMAP.md` | One sequenced row for the migration and the fleet re-pin visit; the repository keeps a roadmap, and the visit outlives this commit. The standing instruction at `ROADMAP.md:58-59` is unaffected. |\n| `src/core/compilers.ts:1508` | The `nameToHostArtifacts` `@example` asserts `AGENTS.md` is a host artifact. That fence becomes false and `tests/guides.test.ts` executes it. Replace the witness with a path that stays, such as `.claude/settings.json`. |\n| `PROPOSAL.md` | Retired at acceptance. Its product truth lands in `guides/scaffold.md`, its measured motivation lands in the commit message, and the file is copied to `.orkestrel/scaffold/` and deleted from the package tree. A published package's tree is its product, and a campaign artifact is not part of it. |\n\n## 10. Types-first order and units\n\nTypes first is cheap here because no type changes. The order is: membership, then the artifact that fills the vacated destinations, then the consumers that read membership, then the proofs, then the prose.\n\nThe campaign's exit criterion — the capabilities whose closure ends it, each ending implemented, repaired, retained, or intentionally excluded:\n\n1. Plan membership splits from staged membership; ambient paths leave the target plan and stay in `dist/host` and `host.json`.\n2. The pointer pair is planned, content-owned, and names the sibling checkout and the correct `dist/host` storage paths, with every `@` inside backticks.\n3. The online audit stops reading and fetching ambient paths.\n4. `audit` reports superseded ambient copies present in a target, and no verb deletes them.\n5. The vendored policy suite passes on a post-migration target shape, and the scaffold-only family claim is re-homed to a scaffold-only always-run proof.\n6. `host.json` and `dist/host` are provably unmoved.\n7. Guide, README, roadmap, and every executed fence agree with what shipped; `PROPOSAL.md` is retired.\n8. Scaffold bumps and publishes; each target re-pins, repairs, sweeps, and proves its gates green.\n\n---\n\n# Alternatives\n\n**Vendor the pointer pair, as `PROPOSAL.md:90-92` writes it.** `HOST_PATHS` entries gain a `source` field so `AGENTS.md` stages from a separate `host/pointers/AGENTS.md` file. Cost: the frozen string list becomes an object list, every consumer of it changes, `host.json` and `dist/host` churn and `tests/config.test.ts` regenerates, and `stageHost:1454-1460` refuses the result outright — scaffold's own `AGENTS.md` and the pointer both project to the storage name `AGENTS.md`, so the collision guard fires and a second mapping layer exists only to get past it. The template path reaches the same repair semantics through a mechanism the package already runs for `README.md`. The design wins.\n\n**Split `HOST_PATHS` into two frozen lists — a staged list and a planned list.** Cost: two membership declarations that can disagree, with no mechanism to notice. A path added to staging and forgotten in planning is a file in `dist/host` no target receives, and the reverse is a planned path `#expand` refuses at write time in a consumer's terminal. \"Derive state\" forbids the second label precisely for this. Declaring the subset and filtering derives the planned set from one source. The design wins.\n\n---\n\n# Units\n\nEvery writing unit runs in the main checkout, one writer at a time, from a clean committed baseline. The Codex bench is dark (CLI absent, recorded at session start), so objective implementation routes to the Opus `implementer` as the recorded substitution rather than to `sol`.\n\n**U1 — Membership split.** Role `implementer`, engine Opus 5.\nOwns `src/core/constants.ts`, `src/core/helpers.ts`, `tests/src/core/helpers.test.ts`. Depends on nothing.\nAccepts when: `npm run check:src:core` is clean; `matchesAmbientPath('.claude/rules/names.md')` is true and `matchesAmbientPath('.claude/agents/orkestrel.md')`, `matchesAmbientPath('.agents/transports/codex.md')`, and `matchesAmbientPath('.claude/settings.json')` are false; `selectHostPaths(HOST_PATHS, 'router')` omits every `AMBIENT_PATHS` member and retains `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/agents`, `.agents/templates`, `.agents/transports`, and `scripts/deps.sh`; `git diff --exit-code host.json` reports no change; `npm run test:src:core` green.\n\n**U2 — Pointer templates and the docs compiler.** Role `implementer`, engine Opus 5.\nOwns `src/core/templates.ts`, `src/core/compilers.ts`, `tests/src/core/compilers.test.ts`, `tests/src/server/helpers.test.ts`. Depends on U1.\nAccepts when: a compiled plan for `router` carries `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` once each, with `origin: 'template'`, `ownership: 'content'`, `group: 'docs'`; both bodies contain `../scaffold/AGENTS.md`, `../scaffold/.agents/orchestration.md`, `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/AGENTS.md`, and `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/agents/orchestration.md`; a test asserts every `@` in each body sits between backticks; a `src:server` test asserts `pathToStorage('.agents/orchestration.md')` equals `agents/orchestration.md` and that the pointer body names that exact string; the `nameToHostArtifacts` `@example` no longer claims `AGENTS.md`; `npm run test:src:core` and `npm run test:src:server` green.\n\n**U3 — CLI overlay filter and superseded advisory.** Role `implementer`, engine Opus 5.\nOwns `src/bin/CLI.ts`, `src/bin/types.ts` (read-only expected), `tests/src/bin/CLI.test.ts`. Depends on U1.\nAccepts when: the online overlay path list excludes every ambient path; `audit` against a fixture target holding `.claude/rules/names.md` returns one question naming that path and prescribing removal; `repair` and `overwrite` against the same fixture raise no such question and leave `.claude/rules` intact; `npm run test:src:bin` green.\n\n**U4 — Policy re-scope.** Role `implementer`, engine Opus 5.\nOwns `tests/policy.test.ts`, `tests/setupPolicy.ts` (fixture data only), `tests/src/server/helpers.test.ts`. Depends on U1.\nAccepts when: the reach assertion names `.claude/settings.json`; the vendored file contains no reference to `orkestrel-falsify`; a `src:server` test asserts the committed `host.json` carries entries at storage `agents/skills/orkestrel-falsify/SKILL.md` and `claude/rules/names.md`; a fixture case shaped like a post-migration target reports no violations from `inspectPolicyWorkspace`; a negative control fixture that drops `.claude/settings.json` still reports the violation it names; `npm run test:policy` green.\n\n**U5 — Documentation and retirement.** Role `implementer`, engine Opus 5 — documentation voice is subjective work.\nOwns `guides/scaffold.md`, `README.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `PROPOSAL.md` (deletion), `tests/guides.test.ts`. Depends on U1 through U4.\nAccepts when: `npm run test:guides` green; the surface table carries `AMBIENT_PATHS` and `matchesAmbientPath`; the guide transcribes both pointer bodies and the transcription equals the compiled artifacts byte for byte; no README sentence describes instruction files as written into targets; `PROPOSAL.md` is absent from the tree and present under `.orkestrel/scaffold/`.\n\n**U6 — Adversarial audit.** Roles `reviewer` (Opus 5) and the objective lane. The objective lane's default engine GPT-5.6 Sol is dark, so it runs on the user-directed substitute Cursor Grok (`cursor-grok-4.6-high`) through the bench CLI, with its journal path and session id returned; its verdicts are proposals the Orchestrator verifies. Dispatch `checker` (Grok, then the ladder) for the mechanical rows — path membership, guide parity, fence transcription. Depends on U1 through U5.\nAccepts when: every numbered claim carries a per-claim verdict with evidence, and the verdict file records any lane that did not run.\n\n**U7 — Gates.** Role `verifier`, engine Sonnet. Depends on U6.\nAccepts when: `format:check`, `lint:check`, `check`, `build`, and `test` each report their real exit status, read bare.\n\n**U8 — Fleet visit and release.** Role `builder`, engine Sonnet, one repository at a time, under the `orkestrel-publish` skill. Depends on U7.\nAccepts when: scaffold's vendored surface change is published; each target re-pins `@orkestrel/scaffold`, runs `repair`, removes the superseded ambient paths in the same commit, and reports its own gates green; a Claude session attaching scaffold plus one target reads a memory total consistent with one canonical copy, recorded from the context panel.\n\n---\n\n# Tensions\n\nNamed for the other lane to challenge.\n\n- **The pointer is a template, not a vendored member.** This departs from `PROPOSAL.md:90-92`. I argue the storage-name collision guard makes the vendored form impossible without a new mapping layer. If the other lane finds a cheap vendored form, its advantage is that `repair`'s digest bookkeeping stays uniform.\n- **`.agents/` splits.** `orchestration.md` and `skills/` leave; `templates/` and `transports/` stay. Coherent by the ambient discriminant, ugly to look at, and it means a target holds a partial `.agents/` tree that reads as an incomplete migration.\n- **Role files stay, skills leave.** A target can dispatch `planner` but cannot invoke `orkestrel-falsify` by name. The asymmetry is forced by the twin-directory law, not chosen.\n- **`.claude/rules/**` leaves as a family**, including rules with `paths` frontmatter that load only on a matching read and therefore cost nothing ambient. Splitting the family by frontmatter would save a little context accuracy and cost the Rule map's coherence. I chose coherence.\n- **Advisory instead of a sweep verb.** If the fleet visit skips the removal, the multiplier survives and only an `audit` question records it. A verb would guarantee the sweep and create a permanent deletion capability over paths scaffold does not claim.\n- **`AMBIENT_PATHS` over `INSTRUCTION_PATHS`.** \"Instruction\" is the word `PROPOSAL.md` uses and it is wrong for this set, because role files are instructions too. \"Ambient\" states the property. It risks confusion with an ambient TypeScript declaration.\n- **The storage paths are written by hand in the pointer prose** and pinned by a test comparing them against `pathToStorage`. Deriving them instead would mean moving `pathToStorage` from server into core, which moves a published export between barrels. I chose the pin.\n- **`PROPOSAL.md` is deleted at acceptance.** Justified by the campaign-artifact rule, and it removes the most readable statement of why this exists from the place a reader would look.\n- **The `orkestrel-falsify` claim moves to an assertion over `host.json`.** It is a stronger claim in a less obvious place, and a reader looking for \"is the skill family complete\" will no longer find it in the policy suite.\n\n---\n\n# Risks\n\n- **A target-rooted Claude session cannot reach the pointer's targets.** The pointer tells an agent to read `../scaffold/AGENTS.md`, which is outside the session's working directory and may trip an access prompt or a denial. Settles with: one live session rooted in a target with scaffold as a sibling, reading `../scaffold/AGENTS.md` and recording whether the Read succeeded without a prompt. Take this measurement before U2 fixes the prose, because a denial makes the `node_modules` form the primary and the sibling form the fallback rather than the reverse.\n- **An agent never follows the pointer.** Nothing enforces the Read. A session that treats a short `AGENTS.md` as the whole contract works without the rules and never says so. Settles with: a dispatched unit in a migrated target, briefed only through the pointer, judged on whether its output obeys a rule stated only in `.claude/rules/names.md`.\n- **`node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold` is absent or stale.** The fallback assumes an installed devDependency and a version whose `dist/host` matches the checkout's intent. A target on an older pin reads older law and cannot tell. Settles with: reading `package.json` `files` (`package.json:25` ships `dist/host`) and confirming each target's declared range, then deciding whether the pointer must state the version it expects.\n- **The design adds a `dist/host` consumer the code cannot see.** Until now `dist/host` was read by `audit`, `repair`, and `catalog`. It is now read by hand, by an agent, at storage paths derived by a function in another environment. Any future change to `pathToStorage` silently breaks every pointer in the fleet. Settles with: the cross-mechanism pin in U2, and a note in the `pathToStorage` `@remarks` naming the pointer as a consumer.\n- **The cloud harness may load more per attached root than the four measured files.** If it also loads `.claude/agents/*` bodies or `.claude/settings.json`, the retained set is part of the multiplier and this ruling on the discriminant is wrong at its edges. Settles with: the before-and-after context panel reading in U8. My design does not depend on `claudeMdExcludes` or on per-root rule loading, so those unknowns stay out of scope.\n- **A target's `overwrite` becomes quieter than the operator expects.** Ambient directories stop being owned roots, so foreign files there are no longer reported or deleted. Someone relying on `overwrite` to clean a harness tree gets silence. Settles with: the U3 fixture asserting exactly that, and the guide's ownership section stating it.\n- **`tests/setupServer.ts` `HOST_DIRECTORY_PATHS` already omits `.agents/templates` and `.agents/transports`** while `HOST_PATHS` declares them as directories. The fixture is self-consistent, so no test fails, but it means the fixture models a checkout scaffold's own tree does not have.
